Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. ````text
1	---

16	
17	# Paper Visualization — 学术图表设计与生成
18	
19	> **核心理念**：好图不是"画得对"，是"回答一个问题"。每张图动笔前先问：**这张图要让读者看到什么？**
20	>
21	> **质量底线**：每张图必须达到 NeurIPS/ICML/EMNLP best paper 的视觉水准——高级感、专业感、精致感。绝不接受 matplotlib 默认样式的廉价感。
22	
23	本 skill 分五层：
24	
25	- **L0 学习同行** — 设计前看几张优秀同类型论文图，比读任何原则都有效
26	- **L1 防丑原则** — 底线，所有图必须遵守
27	- **L2 叙事原则** — 让图有说服力（区分"数据图"和"论证图"）
28	- **L3 Pattern 库** — 具体技法目录，按 `design_notes` 选用
29	- **L4 工具路由 + 模板** — paperbanana vs matplotlib，常用统计图模板
30	
31	---
32	
33	## L0 学习同行（设计前的功课）
34	
35	你的子领域里，**最被引用、最被记住的论文是怎么画图的？** 在动笔之前花点时间去看一眼——这个动作的回报远高于读完下面的 L1-L4。
36	
37	### 为什么这一步是重要的
38	
39	- 同领域读者已经被这些论文的视觉语言"训练"过——遵循惯例 = 降低读者认知负担
40	- 顶会论文经历过 reviewer 严苛审查，能登出来的图基本说明它的 narrative 设计是被接受的
41	- 抽象原则告诉你"用 fill_between 表示发散"，但好论文告诉你"在这种数据上，发散到底应该画多淡才合适"
42	- 一个领域往往有它特有的图表惯例（如校准研究的 reliability diagram、scaling 研究的 log-log curve、RL 的 learning curve with seed bands），不看就不会用
43	
44	### 什么时候值得投入
45	
46	判断权交给你。一些可参考的信号：
47	
48	- **值得多花时间**：会决定论文成败的图（hero figure、main result、新颖的实验类型）
49	- **不太值得**：常规 supplementary 图、loss curve 这类标准化已经很高的图
50	- **强烈建议看**：你打算画一种自己不熟悉的图，或者数据形态是这个领域少见的
51	
52	### 怎么看
53	
54	工具：`paper-image-extractor` skill 可以从 arXiv source 或 PDF 提取参考论文里的 figure。你已经有 `literature_map.yaml` 里的论文清单作为起点。
55	
56	看的时候问自己（不是机械检查清单，是真的去理解）：
57	
58	- 这张图回答了什么问题？读者一眼能 get 到吗？
59	- 它用了哪些叙事层（background band / 参考线 / 概念曲线 / fill 区域）？这些层在我的数据上有对应物吗？
60	- 它的颜色和 marker 怎么承载语义？我的图能不能借鉴这套映射？
61	- 它有什么是这篇论文特有的（与他们的具体数据 / claim 绑死）？这些不要照搬。
62	- 如果让我重画它的图，我会怎么改？为什么作者没这么做？
63	
64	### 怎么把学到的传下去
65	
66	把你看完后的判断**直接写进对应 fig 的 `design_notes`**，用人话不用模板。例子：
67	
68	```yaml
69	design_notes:
70	  - "学 GPTQ Fig 4：用 log-scale bit-width + dashed FP16 baseline + 不同 marker shape 分模型族"
71	  - "Pattern A（regime band）划分 in-domain / OOD 两段"
72	  - "AWQ Fig 2 那种 hatching 区分 outlier 不适用我们的数据，跳过"
73	```
74	
75	Worker 拿到这种 design_notes 就知道既要参考 GPTQ 的 layout 又要用 Pattern A，能直接落实成代码。
76	
77	### 边界
78	
79	- 不要复刻，要消化。直接抄一张图既没用（数据不一样）也有风险（与他们的 claim 强绑定）
80	- 不要看太多。3 张就够，多了 Paper Agent 会陷入"该听谁的"
81	- 看不到原图（提取失败 / arXiv 无 source）就跳过，不要在这里卡住流程
82	
83	---
84	
85	## L1 防丑原则（底线，不可违反）
86	
87	来自 Tufte 和 Luís Cruz 16 条公认规范。Agent 默认遵守，无需逐条思考。
88	
89	1. **Self-explanatory** — 不看正文就能看懂：title/caption + 所有轴有 label + 单位
90	2. **去 chartjunk** — 不要 3D、不要背景框、不要无意义网格、不要 dual y-axis、不要 pie/donut chart
91	3. **直接标注 > Legend** — 能直接标在曲线/柱旁就不用 legend；legend 必要时不遮数据
92	4. **颜色/形状/纹理只用于 data variation** — 不用颜色装饰，不用相同颜色表示不同数据
93	5. **Meaningful baseline** — bar chart 必须从 0 起；scatter 可以非 0
94	6. **文字水平** — label 不要旋转/垂直；类别名太长 → 用 horizontal bar chart
95	7. **Sort for comparison** — 柱状图按数值排序，便于对比（除非有自然顺序如时间）
96	8. **字号一致** — 用 mm 计算 figsize（`ONE_MM = 1/25.4`），不在 LaTeX 里 `\includegraphics[scale=...]` resize
97	9. **色盲友好** — 默认 Okabe-Ito；红/绿区分时变 saturation；考虑 grayscale 打印
98	10. **Bold 只用于强调** — 不要全图加粗；highlight 留给 takeaway
99	11. **Line plot 只用于有序数据** — 类别变量用 bar
100	12. **单 x 单 y 轴** — 必须双轴时改成 multi-panel
101	13. **300 DPI、PDF 矢量、savefig.bbox=tight、去掉 top/right spines**
102	
103	---
104	
105	## L2 叙事原则（让图变成论证）
106	
107	L1 让图"不丑"，L2 让图"有说服力"。这一层是 Agent 自主画图时最容易缺失的。
108	
109	### 原则 1：每张图回答一个明确问题
110	
111	动笔前用一句话写下："This figure shows that ___."
112	
113	如果写不出来，就不要画。如果写出来的 takeaway 不是论文的 claim，就要重新设计。
114	
115	**反例**：`"Accuracy across methods"` — 这只是个标题，不是问题
116	**正例**：`"FP16 baseline 在小模型上比量化版高 5pp，但 70B 模型上差距消失"` — 这是一张图要说服读者相信的事
117	
118	`visualization_plan.{fig_id}.narrative_intent` 字段写这一句。
119	
120	### 原则 2：颜色绑定语义而非数据顺序
121	
122	错的做法：`colors = ['blue', 'orange', 'green', 'red']` 按数据顺序分配。
123	对的做法：`BN → 蓝色（始终）`、`non-BN → 橙色（始终）`、`collapse/danger → vermillion (#D55E00)`、`safe/baseline → blue (#0072B2)`。
124	
125	整篇论文 6 张图，BN 永远蓝、collapse 永远红——零认知负担，读者一眼建立映射。
126	
127	### 原则 3：信息层级用 visual variable 表达
128	
129	数据的"质量"和"分类"应该可见，不应该藏在 caption 里：
130	
131	| 信息 | Visual 表达 |
132	|------|------------|
133	| n < 50 低置信度点 | hollow marker + 标注 `n=` |
134	| Out-of-sample / pending 数据 | hollow + 不同 marker shape（如 diamond）+ "OOS" label |
135	| Collapse / 异常类别 | hatching（斜线填充）|
136	| 主结果 vs 次要结果 | 主用饱和色，次要用 muted 色或 alpha |
137	
138	### 原则 4：加叙事层，不只画数据
139	
140	数据是图的基础，叙事层是让读者"读懂"的关键。常见叙事层：
141	
142	- **Background bands** — `axhspan/axvspan` 标定性区域（"collapse zone"、"safe zone"、"Regime A/B/C"）
143	- **Fill_between** — 显示两条曲线发散区域（alpha 极低如 0.06，不抢眼但有面积）
144	- **Reference lines** — 锚定真实值（如 dashed line 标"actual W3A16 accuracy"）
145	- **Equivalence band** — `axvspan` gradient fade 标 +/-1pp 等价区间
146	- **Concept curve** — 完全合成的曲线（如 cosine trajectory）建立理论直觉
147	
148	这些都不来自数据，但帮读者"看到"数据想说什么。
149	
150	### 原则 5：Subtitle / panel label 写 insight 不写描述
151	
152	错：`"Layer 6 comparison across three models"`
153	对：`"Same relative position, opposite function"`
154	
155	错：`"Reliability diagram"`
156	对：`"Quantization shifts confidence but not accuracy"`
157	
158	副标题是免费的 takeaway，不要浪费。
159	
160	### 原则 6：Multi-panel 每个面板独立回答一个 sub-question
161	
162	不要做"主图 + 装饰小图"。每个 panel 必须有独立的信息价值。
163	
164	如果一个 panel 想不出独立的问题，要么删掉，要么合并到主 panel。
165	
166	布局上：非对称比例（如 1.4:1）让视觉重心对齐叙事重心。
167	
168	### 原则 7：所有图至少 3 轮迭代，Hero figure 至少 5 轮
169	
170	第一版画出来基本不能直接用。计划好预算：先确定数据和布局，再调样式标注，最后 polish。不要指望一次到位。
171	
172	### Hero Figure 定义
173	
174	Hero Figure = Figure 1 + 主结果图（main result figure）。这两类图对论文第一印象影响最大，必须投入更多迭代。
175	
176	---
177	
178	## L3 Pattern 库（按 `design_notes` 选用）
179	
180	按"图要回答的问题"选 pattern。每个 pattern 给出**何时用**、**怎么用**（具体代码或 matplotlib API）。
181	
182	### Pattern A：定性区域 / regime bands
183	
184	**何时用**：x 或 y 轴有自然分段（regime、phase、severity tier）。
185	
186	```python
187	# horizontal bands
188	for lo, hi, color, label in [(0, 1, '#EBF5FB', 'Regime A'), (1, 5, '#EAFAF1', 'Regime B')]:
189	    ax.axhspan(lo, hi, alpha=0.25, color=color, zorder=0)
190	    ax.text(x_label_pos, (lo+hi)/2, label, va='center', fontsize=9, color='gray')
191	
192	# 单一 collapse zone
193	ax.axhspan(-5, 5, color='#FDEDEC', alpha=0.5, zorder=0)
194	ax.text(0.02, 2.5, 'collapse zone', fontsize=8, color='#ccaaaa', fontstyle='italic')
195	```
196	
197	### Pattern B：发散面积 fill_between
198	
199	**何时用**：两组数据从某点开始发散，想让"发散程度"可见。
200	
201	```python
202	# alpha 0.06 极淡，不抢眼但有面积
203	ax.fill_between(x_range, curve_high, curve_low, alpha=0.06, color='#555555', zorder=1)
204	```
205	
206	### Pattern C：参考线锚定真实值
207	
208	**何时用**：合成 / 缩放后的 x 轴需要锚定到真实物理量。
209	
210	```python
211	ax.axhline(y=actual_value, color=color, linestyle=':', linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.3)
212	t = ax.text(x_pos, actual_value+1, 'actual W3A16', fontsize=7, color=color, alpha=0.5)
213	t.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])  # 防遮挡
214	```
215	
216	### Pattern D：信息层级 marker（hollow vs solid）
217	
218	**何时用**：同一图中有不同置信度 / OOS / pending 数据。
219	
220	```python
221	# 实心 = n >= 50, 空心 = n < 50
222	if n < 50:
223	    ax.plot(x, y, marker='o', markerfacecolor='white', markeredgecolor=color,
224	            markeredgewidth=1.2, markersize=6)
225	    ax.annotate(f'n={n}', (x, y), xytext=(0, 7), textcoords='offset points',
226	                fontsize=5, color=color, fontstyle='italic')
227	```
228	
229	### Pattern E：Wilson CI / bootstrap 不确定性
230	
231	**何时用**：accuracy / proportion 数据需要置信区间。
232	
233	```python
234	def wilson_ci(p, n, z=1.96):
235	    denom = 1 + z**2 / n
236	    center = (p + z**2 / (2*n)) / denom
237	    half = z * np.sqrt((p*(1-p) + z**2/(4*n)) / n) / denom
238	    return max(0, center - half), min(1, center + half)
239	```
240	
241	### Pattern F：Per-label offset dict（解决标注重叠）
242	
243	**何时用**：scatter 上多个点需要标注且自动布局重叠。
244	
245	```python
246	label_offsets = {
247	    'Qwen3-8B':  (6, -12),   # 向下避让
248	    'Qwen3-4B':  (-55, 4),   # 向左避让
249	    'Llama-8B':  (6, 6),
250	}
251	for name, (x, y) in data.items():
252	    ox, oy = label_offsets.get(name, (6, 6))
253	    ax.annotate(name, (x, y), xytext=(ox, oy), textcoords='offset points', fontsize=7)
254	```
255	
256	### Pattern G：Hatching 加分类维度
257	
258	**何时用**：已用颜色表示一个维度，再加一个二元维度（如 collapse vs not）。
259	
260	```python
261	for is_collapse, bar in zip(collapse_flags, bars):
262	    if is_collapse:
263	        bar.set_hatch('///')
264	        bar.set_alpha(0.7)
265	```
266	
267	### Pattern H：Multi-panel 非对称布局
268	
269	**何时用**：主图 + 辅助 panel；或两个等重要 panel 但视觉重心需要倾斜。
270	
271	```python
272	fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(6.5, 3.2),
273	                          gridspec_kw={'width_ratios': [1.4, 1]})
274	# 主面板 1.4x 宽，副面板 1x；视觉重心在左
275	```
276	
277	### Pattern I：Broken axis（异常值或对数 0）
278	
279	**何时用**：数据范围跨度大，或对数轴需要表示 0。
280	
281	```python
282	d = 0.012
283	kwargs = dict(transform=ax.transAxes, color='black', clip_on=False, linewidth=0.6)
284	ax.plot((1-d, 1+d), (-d, +d), **kwargs)  # 右下角斜线
285	ax.plot((1-d, 1+d), (1-d, 1+d), **kwargs)  # 右上角斜线
286	```
287	
288	### Pattern J：Path-effect 文字描边
289	
290	**何时用**：标注必须放在数据点 / colored region 上方。
291	
292	```python
293	import matplotlib.patheffects as pe
294	t = ax.text(x, y, 'label', fontsize=8, color='red')
295	t.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])
296	```
297	
298	### Pattern K：Concept curve（建立直觉）
299	
300	**何时用**：论文核心 claim 是定性现象，需要先让读者理解理论预期。
301	
302	```python
303	# 完全合成的 cosine trajectory，不是实验数据
304	x = np.linspace(0, 1, 100)
305	trajectory = baseline * (1 - np.cos(np.pi * x)) / 2
306	ax.plot(x, trajectory, linewidth=2, solid_capstyle='round')
307	ax.annotate('gap eliminated', (1.0, final_y), color='#388E3C',
308	            fontstyle='italic', fontsize=9)
309	```
310	
311	### Pattern L：Forest plot（meta-analysis 风格）
312	
313	**何时用**：多个子条件的 effect size + CI 对比，加 inline 统计数字。
314	
315	3-column GridSpec：(1) 标签 (2) CI 区间图 (3) 统计数字（mean, CI, p-value）
316	
317	每行：dot + horizontal line(CI) + 数字 inline。等价带用半透明 axvspan。
318	
319	### 概念图 L1 防丑原则（7 条）
320	
321	概念图（架构图、流程图、framework 示意）的视觉底线，与统计图 L1 互补。
322	
323	1. **Flow direction 一致** — 全图统一左→右或上→下，不混合方向
324	2. **Box 规格统一** — 同层级 box 尺寸、圆角、边框粗细一致
325	3. **箭头样式统一** — 不混用多种箭头类型（除非有语义区分）
326	4. **层级清晰** — 不同抽象层用缩进/嵌套/背景色区分
327	5. **密度控制** — 单张图 ≤15 个 box，超过则拆分或分组
328	6. **Label 完整** — 每个 box 和箭头都有文字说明
329	7. **无装饰性元素** — 不加纯装饰图标、渐变、3D 效果
330	
331	### 概念图 L2 叙事原则（6 条）
332	
333	让概念图不只是"画了个框图"，而是"引导读者理解系统"。
334	
335	1. **视觉锚点** — 核心贡献用饱和色/大字/加粗边框突出，其余 muted
336	2. **阅读路径** — 沿箭头方向自然读完全图，不需要跳跃
337	3. **对比布局** — 左右并列展示 before/after 或 baseline/ours
338	4. **分组嵌套** — 用浅色背景框将相关组件分组（如 encoder/decoder）
339	5. **箭头语义** — 实线=数据流，虚线=控制流/可选，粗线=关键路径
340	6. **信息层级** — 主路径用饱和色+大字，辅助信息用灰色+小字
341	
342	### 概念图 L3 Pattern 库
343	
344	| Pattern | 名称 | 适用场景 |
345	|---------|------|---------|
346	| P-A | 线性流程 | 数据处理 pipeline、推理流程 |
347	| P-B | 层级嵌套 | 模型架构（encoder/decoder/attention） |
348	| P-C | 对比并列 | baseline vs ours、before/after |
349	| P-D | 分支汇聚 | 多输入融合、ensemble |
350	| P-E | 循环 | 训练循环、迭代优化 |
351	| P-F | 诊断链 | 消融实验逻辑、排错流程 |
352	| P-G | 三面板综合 | 概念+数据+结论（常用于 Figure 1） |
353	
354	### 概念图 Checklist（7 项）
355	
356	- [ ] Flow direction 全图统一（左→右或上→下）
357	- [ ] Box 规格一致（同层级尺寸、圆角、边框相同）
358	- [ ] 视觉层级清晰（核心突出，辅助 muted）
359	- [ ] 核心贡献在视觉上最突出
360	- [ ] 箭头语义一致（实线/虚线/粗细有意义）
361	- [ ] 留白充足（box 之间不拥挤）
362	- [ ] 跨图风格一致（配色、字号、线宽与论文其他图统一）
363	
364	---
365	
366	## L4 工具路由 + 模板
367	
368	### 路由规则
369	
370	| 场景 | 工具 | 原因 |
371	|------|------|------|
372	| 概念图（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana（Gemini）** | matplotlib 手写 boxes/arrows 极痛苦 |
373	| 统计图（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **matplotlib** | 确定性、可控、有模板 |
374	| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **matplotlib** | 不依赖外部 API |
375	
376	项目级偏好：`config/projects/{name}.yaml` 的 `visualization.preferred_tool` 可覆盖。
377	
378	### Matplotlib 通用样式（rcParams 标准）
379	
380	```python
381	import matplotlib
382	matplotlib.use('Agg')
383	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
384	import matplotlib.patheffects as pe
385	import numpy as np
386	
387	plt.rcParams.update({
388	    'font.family': 'DejaVu Sans',
389	    'font.size': 11, 'axes.titlesize': 13, 'axes.labelsize': 12,
390	    'xtick.labelsize': 10, 'ytick.labelsize': 10, 'legend.fontsize': 10,
391	    'figure.dpi': 300, 'savefig.dpi': 300,
392	    'savefig.bbox': 'tight', 'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,
393	    'axes.spines.top': False, 'axes.spines.right': False,
394	    'pdf.fonttype': 42, 'ps.fonttype': 42,  # TrueType 嵌入
395	    'lines.linewidth': 1.8,
396	})
397	
398	# 色盲友好语义色板（Okabe-Ito）
399	SEMANTIC_COLORS = {
400	    'baseline':   '#0072B2',  # blue - safe / mild
401	    'treatment':  '#E69F00',  # orange - intervention
402	    'collapse':   '#D55E00',  # vermillion - danger / catastrophic
403	    'safe':       '#009E73',  # green - good outcome
404	    'neutral':    '#999999',  # gray - reference / control
405	}
406	```
407	
408	### 常用模板（仅作起点，需按 narrative_intent 改造）
409	
410	详细执行模板见 `skills/global/draw-paper-figure.skill.md`：
411	- Reliability Diagram（校准图）
412	- ECE Heatmap（方法 × 数据集 ΔECE）
413	- T* Scatter Plot（任务 Regime 可视化）
414	
415	**重要**：这些模板是起点不是终点。每张图根据 `narrative_intent` 必须加 L3 中的相关 pattern，不能直接套用。
416	
417	### 主图（Figure 1）特别提示
418	
419	大多数 ML 论文的 Figure 1 是概念图/架构图/方法概览——正是 PaperBanana 的强项。除非 Figure 1 是纯数值结果对比（如 Pareto frontier），否则优先用 PaperBanana。Figure 1 的 `narrative_intent` 必须与 `writing_plan.yaml` 的 `meta.core_claim` 直接关联。
420	
421	### PaperBanana 用法
422	
423	见 `skills/global/paper-banana.skill.md`。意图描述时直接引用 `narrative_intent`，并把 L3 中的 pattern 名写进 intent（如 "with collapse zone background band, hollow markers for n<50"）。
424	
425	---
426	
427	## 给 Paper Agent 的填写规则
428	
429	规划 `visualization_plan` 时，对每张图填：
430	
431	```yaml
432	- fig_id: fig2_main_result
433	  type: statistical
434	  tool: matplotlib
435	  data_source: "registry: exp_001, exp_002"
436	  placement: experiments
437	
438	  # 新字段（必填）
439	  narrative_intent: "Quantized models match FP16 on accuracy but degrade calibration; ECE gap widens with bits→2."
440	  design_notes:
441	    - "Pattern A: regime bands for bits=8/4/3"
442	    - "Pattern C: dashed line for FP16 ECE baseline"
443	    - "Pattern D: hollow markers for n<50 data points"
444	
445	  aspect_ratio: "4:3"
446	```
447	
448	`narrative_intent`：一句话，主动语态，描述读者应该相信的事。
449	
450	`design_notes`：从 L3 选 0-4 个 pattern，给出选用理由。可以为空（说明默认模板足够）。
451	
452	---
453	
454	## 提交前 Checklist（以下为统计图专用）
455	
456	L1 部分（防丑底线）：
457	- [ ] 所有轴有 label + 单位
458	- [ ] 无 dual y-axis、无 3D、无 pie
459	- [ ] 颜色绑定语义、色盲友好
460	- [ ] Bar chart 从 0 起（如有）
461	- [ ] 字号 ≥ 10pt（缩到 column width 后仍可读）
462	- [ ] PDF 矢量、300 DPI
463	
464	L2 部分（叙事强度）：
465	- [ ] 能用一句话写出 figure takeaway（应等于 `narrative_intent`）
466	- [ ] Subtitle / panel label 是 insight，不是描述
467	- [ ] 数据质量差异在视觉上可见（n、OOS、pending 等）
468	- [ ] 至少考虑过是否需要 background band / fill_between / reference line（不一定要加）
469	
470	L3 部分（高级感——不可跳过）：
471	- [ ] 不像 matplotlib gallery 示例（无默认灰背景、无粗网格、无 tab10 色板）
472	- [ ] 配色 muted 和谐，不刺眼，不过度饱和
473	- [ ] 线宽、字号、间距全图统一
474	- [ ] 元素对齐严格（bars 底部对齐、labels 水平对齐）
475	- [ ] 留白充足（元素之间不拥挤）
476	- [ ] 与论文全文其他图的风格一致
477	- [ ] 终极判断：这张图放在 NeurIPS best paper 里违和吗？
478	
````

visualization\n- paper\n- matplotlib\n- paperbanana\nversion: 5\nroles:\n- agent\n- paper_agent\n---\n\n# Paper Visualization — 学术图表设计与生成\n\n> **核心理念**：好图不是\"画得对\"，是\"回答一个问题\"。每张图动笔前先问：**这张图要让读者看到什么？**\n>\n> **质量底线**：每张图必须达到 NeurIPS/ICML/EMNLP best paper 的视觉水准——高级感、专业感、精致感。绝不接受 matplotlib 默认样式的廉价感。\n\n本 skill 分五层：\n\n- **L0 学习同行** — 设计前看几张优秀同类型论文图，比读任何原则都有效\n- **L1 防丑原则** — 底线，所有图必须遵守\n- **L2 叙事原则** — 让图有说服力（区分\"数据图\"和\"论证图\"）\n- **L3 Pattern 库** — 具体技法目录，按 `design_notes` 选用\n- **L4 工具路由 + 模板** — paperbanana vs matplotlib，常用统计图模板\n\n---\n\n## L0 学习同行（设计前的功课）\n\n你的子领域里，**最被引用、最被记住的论文是怎么画图的？** 在动笔之前花点时间去看一眼——这个动作的回报远高于读完下面的 L1-L4。\n\n### 为什么这一步是重要的\n\n- 同领域读者已经被这些论文的视觉语言\"训练\"过——遵循惯例 = 降低读者认知负担\n- 顶会论文经历过 reviewer 严苛审查，能登出来的图基本说明它的 narrative 设计是被接受的\n- 抽象原则告诉你\"用 fill_between 表示发散\"，但好论文告诉你\"在这种数据上，发散到底应该画多淡才合适\"\n- 一个领域往往有它特有的图表惯例（如校准研究的 reliability diagram、scaling 研究的 log-log curve、RL 的 learning curve with seed bands），不看就不会用\n\n### 什么时候值得投入\n\n判断权交给你。一些可参考的信号：\n\n- **值得多花时间**：会决定论文成败的图（hero figure、main result、新颖的实验类型）\n- **不太值得**：常规 supplementary 图、loss curve 这类标准化已经很高的图\n- **强烈建议看**：你打算画一种自己不熟悉的图，或者数据形态是这个领域少见的\n\n### 怎么看\n\n工具：`paper-image-extractor` skill 可以从 arXiv source 或 PDF 提取参考论文里的 figure。你已经有 `literature_map.yaml` 里的论文清单作为起点。\n\n看的时候问自己（不是机械检查清单，是真的去理解）：\n\n- 这张图回答了什么问题？读者一眼能 get 到吗？\n- 它用了哪些叙事层（background band / 参考线 / 概念曲线 / fill 区域）？这些层在我的数据上有对应物吗？\n- 它的颜色和 marker 怎么承载语义？我的图能不能借鉴这套映射？\n- 它有什么是这篇论文特有的（与他们的具体数据 / claim 绑死）？这些不要照搬。\n- 如果让我重画它的图，我会怎么改？为什么作者没这么做？\n\n### 怎么把学到的传下去\n\n把你看完后的判断**直接写进对应 fig 的 `design_notes`**，用人话不用模板。例子：\n\n```yaml\ndesign_notes:\n  - \"学 GPTQ Fig 4：用 log-scale bit-width + dashed FP16 baseline + 不同 marker shape 分模型族\"\n  - \"Pattern A（regime band）划分 in-domain / OOD 两段\"\n  - \"AWQ Fig 2 那种 hatching 区分 outlier 不适用我们的数据，跳过\"\n```\n\nWorker 拿到这种 design_notes 就知道既要参考 GPTQ 的 layout 又要用 Pattern A，能直接落实成代码。\n\n### 边界\n\n- 不要复刻，要消化。直接抄一张图既没用（数据不一样）也有风险（与他们的 claim 强绑定）\n- 不要看太多。3 张就够，多了 Paper Agent 会陷入\"该听谁的\"\n- 看不到原图（提取失败 / arXiv 无 source）就跳过，不要在这里卡住流程\n\n---\n\n## L1 防丑原则（底线，不可违反）\n\n来自 Tufte 和 Luís Cruz 16 条公认规范。Agent 默认遵守，无需逐条思考。\n\n1. **Self-explanatory** — 不看正文就能看懂：title/caption + 所有轴有 label + 单位\n2. **去 chartjunk** — 不要 3D、不要背景框、不要无意义网格、不要 dual y-axis、不要 pie/donut chart\n3. **直接标注 > Legend** — 能直接标在曲线/柱旁就不用 legend；legend 必要时不遮数据\n4. **颜色/形状/纹理只用于 data variation** — 不用颜色装饰，不用相同颜色表示不同数据\n5. **Meaningful baseline** — bar chart 必须从 0 起；scatter 可以非 0\n6. **文字水平** — label 不要旋转/垂直；类别名太长 → 用 horizontal bar chart\n7. **Sort for comparison** — 柱状图按数值排序，便于对比（除非有自然顺序如时间）\n8. **字号一致** — 用 mm 计算 figsize（`ONE_MM = 1/25.4`），不在 LaTeX 里 `\\includegraphics[scale=...]` resize\n9. **色盲友好** — 默认 Okabe-Ito；红/绿区分时变 saturation；考虑 grayscale 打印\n10. **Bold 只用于强调** — 不要全图加粗；highlight 留给 takeaway\n11. **Line plot 只用于有序数据** — 类别变量用 bar\n12. **单 x 单 y 轴** — 必须双轴时改成 multi-panel\n13. **300 DPI、PDF 矢量、savefig.bbox=tight、去掉 top/right spines**\n\n---\n\n## L2 叙事原则（让图变成论证）\n\nL1 让图\"不丑\"，L2 让图\"有说服力\"。这一层是 Agent 自主画图时最容易缺失的。\n\n### 原则 1：每张图回答一个明确问题\n\n动笔前用一句话写下：\"This figure shows that ___.\"\n\n如果写不出来，就不要画。如果写出来的 takeaway 不是论文的 claim，就要重新设计。\n\n**反例**：`\"Accuracy across methods\"` — 这只是个标题，不是问题\n**正例**：`\"FP16 baseline 在小模型上比量化版高 5pp，但 70B 模型上差距消失\"` — 这是一张图要说服读者相信的事\n\n`visualization_plan.{fig_id}.narrative_intent` 字段写这一句。\n\n### 原则 2：颜色绑定语义而非数据顺序\n\n错的做法：`colors = ['blue', 'orange', 'green', 'red']` 按数据顺序分配。\n对的做法：`BN → 蓝色（始终）`、`non-BN → 橙色（始终）`、`collapse/danger → vermillion (#D55E00)`、`safe/baseline → blue (#0072B2)`。\n\n整篇论文 6 张图，BN 永远蓝、collapse 永远红——零认知负担，读者一眼建立映射。\n\n### 原则 3：信息层级用 visual variable 表达\n\n数据的\"质量\"和\"分类\"应该可见，不应该藏在 caption 里：\n\n| 信息 | Visual 表达 |\n|------|------------|\n| n < 50 低置信度点 | hollow marker + 标注 `n=` |\n| Out-of-sample / pending 数据 | hollow + 不同 marker shape（如 diamond）+ \"OOS\" label |\n| Collapse / 异常类别 | hatching（斜线填充）|\n| 主结果 vs 次要结果 | 主用饱和色，次要用 muted 色或 alpha |\n\n### 原则 4：加叙事层，不只画数据\n\n数据是图的基础，叙事层是让读者\"读懂\"的关键。常见叙事层：\n\n- **Background bands** — `axhspan/axvspan` 标定性区域（\"collapse zone\"、\"safe zone\"、\"Regime A/B/C\"）\n- **Fill_between** — 显示两条曲线发散区域（alpha 极低如 0.06，不抢眼但有面积）\n- **Reference lines** — 锚定真实值（如 dashed line 标\"actual W3A16 accuracy\"）\n- **Equivalence band** — `axvspan` gradient fade 标 +/-1pp 等价区间\n- **Concept curve** — 完全合成的曲线（如 cosine trajectory）建立理论直觉\n\n这些都不来自数据，但帮读者\"看到\"数据想说什么。\n\n### 原则 5：Subtitle / panel label 写 insight 不写描述\n\n错：`\"Layer 6 comparison across three models\"`\n对：`\"Same relative position, opposite function\"`\n\n错：`\"Reliability diagram\"`\n对：`\"Quantization shifts confidence but not accuracy\"`\n\n副标题是免费的 takeaway，不要浪费。\n\n### 原则 6：Multi-panel 每个面板独立回答一个 sub-question\n\n不要做\"主图 + 装饰小图\"。每个 panel 必须有独立的信息价值。\n\n如果一个 panel 想不出独立的问题，要么删掉，要么合并到主 panel。\n\n布局上：非对称比例（如 1.4:1）让视觉重心对齐叙事重心。\n\n### 原则 7：所有图至少 3 轮迭代，Hero figure 至少 5 轮\n\n第一版画出来基本不能直接用。计划好预算：先确定数据和布局，再调样式标注，最后 polish。不要指望一次到位。\n\n### Hero Figure 定义\n\nHero Figure = Figure 1 + 主结果图（main result figure）。这两类图对论文第一印象影响最大，必须投入更多迭代。\n\n---\n\n## L3 Pattern 库（按 `design_notes` 选用）\n\n按\"图要回答的问题\"选 pattern。每个 pattern 给出**何时用**、**怎么用**（具体代码或 matplotlib API）。\n\n### Pattern A：定性区域 / regime bands\n\n**何时用**：x 或 y 轴有自然分段（regime、phase、severity tier）。\n\n```python\n# horizontal bands\nfor lo, hi, color, label in [(0, 1, '#EBF5FB', 'Regime A'), (1, 5, '#EAFAF1', 'Regime B')]:\n    ax.axhspan(lo, hi, alpha=0.25, color=color, zorder=0)\n    ax.text(x_label_pos, (lo+hi)/2, label, va='center', fontsize=9, color='gray')\n\n# 单一 collapse zone\nax.axhspan(-5, 5, color='#FDEDEC', alpha=0.5, zorder=0)\nax.text(0.02, 2.5, 'collapse zone', fontsize=8, color='#ccaaaa', fontstyle='italic')\n```\n\n### Pattern B：发散面积 fill_between\n\n**何时用**：两组数据从某点开始发散，想让\"发散程度\"可见。\n\n```python\n# alpha 0.06 极淡，不抢眼但有面积\nax.fill_between(x_range, curve_high, curve_low, alpha=0.06, color='#555555', zorder=1)\n```\n\n### Pattern C：参考线锚定真实值\n\n**何时用**：合成 / 缩放后的 x 轴需要锚定到真实物理量。\n\n```python\nax.axhline(y=actual_value, color=color, linestyle=':', linewidth=0.8, alpha=0.3)\nt = ax.text(x_pos, actual_value+1, 'actual W3A16', fontsize=7, color=color, alpha=0.5)\nt.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])  # 防遮挡\n```\n\n### Pattern D：信息层级 marker（hollow vs solid）\n\n**何时用**：同一图中有不同置信度 / OOS / pending 数据。\n\n```python\n# 实心 = n >= 50, 空心 = n < 50\nif n < 50:\n    ax.plot(x, y, marker='o', markerfacecolor='white', markeredgecolor=color,\n            markeredgewidth=1.2, markersize=6)\n    ax.annotate(f'n={n}', (x, y), xytext=(0, 7), textcoords='offset points',\n                fontsize=5, color=color, fontstyle='italic')\n```\n\n### Pattern E：Wilson CI / bootstrap 不确定性\n\n**何时用**：accuracy / proportion 数据需要置信区间。\n\n```python\ndef wilson_ci(p, n, z=1.96):\n    denom = 1 + z**2 / n\n    center = (p + z**2 / (2*n)) / denom\n    half = z * np.sqrt((p*(1-p) + z**2/(4*n)) / n) / denom\n    return max(0, center - half), min(1, center + half)\n```\n\n### Pattern F：Per-label offset dict（解决标注重叠）\n\n**何时用**：scatter 上多个点需要标注且自动布局重叠。\n\n```python\nlabel_offsets = {\n    'Qwen3-8B':  (6, -12),   # 向下避让\n    'Qwen3-4B':  (-55, 4),   # 向左避让\n    'Llama-8B':  (6, 6),\n}\nfor name, (x, y) in data.items():\n    ox, oy = label_offsets.get(name, (6, 6))\n    ax.annotate(name, (x, y), xytext=(ox, oy), textcoords='offset points', fontsize=7)\n```\n\n### Pattern G：Hatching 加分类维度\n\n**何时用**：已用颜色表示一个维度，再加一个二元维度（如 collapse vs not）。\n\n```python\nfor is_collapse, bar in zip(collapse_flags, bars):\n    if is_collapse:\n        bar.set_hatch('///')\n        bar.set_alpha(0.7)\n```\n\n### Pattern H：Multi-panel 非对称布局\n\n**何时用**：主图 + 辅助 panel；或两个等重要 panel 但视觉重心需要倾斜。\n\n```python\nfig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(6.5, 3.2),\n                          gridspec_kw={'width_ratios': [1.4, 1]})\n# 主面板 1.4x 宽，副面板 1x；视觉重心在左\n```\n\n### Pattern I：Broken axis（异常值或对数 0）\n\n**何时用**：数据范围跨度大，或对数轴需要表示 0。\n\n```python\nd = 0.012\nkwargs = dict(transform=ax.transAxes, color='black', clip_on=False, linewidth=0.6)\nax.plot((1-d, 1+d), (-d, +d), **kwargs)  # 右下角斜线\nax.plot((1-d, 1+d), (1-d, 1+d), **kwargs)  # 右上角斜线\n```\n\n### Pattern J：Path-effect 文字描边\n\n**何时用**：标注必须放在数据点 / colored region 上方。\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\nt = ax.text(x, y, 'label', fontsize=8, color='red')\nt.set_path_effects([pe.withStroke(linewidth=2.5, foreground='white')])\n```\n\n### Pattern K：Concept curve（建立直觉）\n\n**何时用**：论文核心 claim 是定性现象，需要先让读者理解理论预期。\n\n```python\n# 完全合成的 cosine trajectory，不是实验数据\nx = np.linspace(0, 1, 100)\ntrajectory = baseline * (1 - np.cos(np.pi * x)) / 2\nax.plot(x, trajectory, linewidth=2, solid_capstyle='round')\nax.annotate('gap eliminated', (1.0, final_y), color='#388E3C',\n            fontstyle='italic', fontsize=9)\n```\n\n### Pattern L：Forest plot（meta-analysis 风格）\n\n**何时用**：多个子条件的 effect size + CI 对比，加 inline 统计数字。\n\n3-column GridSpec：(1) 标签 (2) CI 区间图 (3) 统计数字（mean, CI, p-value）\n\n每行：dot + horizontal line(CI) + 数字 inline。等价带用半透明 axvspan。\n\n### 概念图 L1 防丑原则（7 条）\n\n概念图（架构图、流程图、framework 示意）的视觉底线，与统计图 L1 互补。\n\n1. **Flow direction 一致** — 全图统一左→右或上→下，不混合方向\n2. **Box 规格统一** — 同层级 box 尺寸、圆角、边框粗细一致\n3. **箭头样式统一** — 不混用多种箭头类型（除非有语义区分）\n4. **层级清晰** — 不同抽象层用缩进/嵌套/背景色区分\n5. **密度控制** — 单张图 ≤15 个 box，超过则拆分或分组\n6. **Label 完整** — 每个 box 和箭头都有文字说明\n7. **无装饰性元素** — 不加纯装饰图标、渐变、3D 效果\n\n### 概念图 L2 叙事原则（6 条）\n\n让概念图不只是\"画了个框图\"，而是\"引导读者理解系统\"。\n\n1. **视觉锚点** — 核心贡献用饱和色/大字/加粗边框突出，其余 muted\n2. **阅读路径** — 沿箭头方向自然读完全图，不需要跳跃\n3. **对比布局** — 左右并列展示 before/after 或 baseline/ours\n4. **分组嵌套** — 用浅色背景框将相关组件分组（如 encoder/decoder）\n5. **箭头语义** — 实线=数据流，虚线=控制流/可选，粗线=关键路径\n6. **信息层级** — 主路径用饱和色+大字，辅助信息用灰色+小字\n\n### 概念图 L3 Pattern 库\n\n| Pattern | 名称 | 适用场景 |\n|---------|------|---------|\n| P-A | 线性流程 | 数据处理 pipeline、推理流程 |\n| P-B | 层级嵌套 | 模型架构（encoder/decoder/attention） |\n| P-C | 对比并列 | baseline vs ours、before/after |\n| P-D | 分支汇聚 | 多输入融合、ensemble |\n| P-E | 循环 | 训练循环、迭代优化 |\n| P-F | 诊断链 | 消融实验逻辑、排错流程 |\n| P-G | 三面板综合 | 概念+数据+结论（常用于 Figure 1） |\n\n### 概念图 Checklist（7 项）\n\n- [ ] Flow direction 全图统一（左→右或上→下）\n- [ ] Box 规格一致（同层级尺寸、圆角、边框相同）\n- [ ] 视觉层级清晰（核心突出，辅助 muted）\n- [ ] 核心贡献在视觉上最突出\n- [ ] 箭头语义一致（实线/虚线/粗细有意义）\n- [ ] 留白充足（box 之间不拥挤）\n- [ ] 跨图风格一致（配色、字号、线宽与论文其他图统一）\n\n---\n\n## L4 工具路由 + 模板\n\n### 路由规则\n\n| 场景 | 工具 | 原因 |\n|------|------|------|\n| 概念图（架构图、流程图、framework 示意） | **PaperBanana（Gemini）** | matplotlib 手写 boxes/arrows 极痛苦 |\n| 统计图（scatter、bar、line、heatmap、forest） | **matplotlib** | 确定性、可控、有模板 |\n| 离线 / 无 GOOGLE_API_KEY | **matplotlib** | 不依赖外部 API |\n\n项目级偏好：`config/projects/{name}.yaml` 的 `visualization.preferred_tool` 可覆盖。\n\n### Matplotlib 通用样式（rcParams 标准）\n\n```python\nimport matplotlib\nmatplotlib.use('Agg')\nimport matplotlib.pyplot as plt\nimport matplotlib.patheffects as pe\nimport numpy as np\n\nplt.rcParams.update({\n    'font.family': 'DejaVu Sans',\n    'font.size': 11, 'axes.titlesize': 13, 'axes.labelsize': 12,\n    'xtick.labelsize': 10, 'ytick.labelsize': 10, 'legend.fontsize': 10,\n    'figure.dpi': 300, 'savefig.dpi': 300,\n    'savefig.bbox': 'tight', 'savefig.pad_inches': 0.05,\n    'axes.spines.top': False, 'axes.spines.right': False,\n    'pdf.fonttype': 42, 'ps.fonttype': 42,  # TrueType 嵌入\n    'lines.linewidth': 1.8,\n})\n\n# 色盲友好语义色板（Okabe-Ito）\nSEMANTIC_COLORS = {\n    'baseline':   '#0072B2',  # blue - safe / mild\n    'treatment':  '#E69F00',  # orange - intervention\n    'collapse':   '#D55E00',  # vermillion - danger / catastrophic\n    'safe':       '#009E73',  # green - good outcome\n    'neutral':    '#999999',  # gray - reference / control\n}\n```\n\n### 常用模板（仅作起点，需按 narrative_intent 改造）\n\n详细执行模板见 `skills/global/draw-paper-figure.skill.md`：\n- Reliability Diagram（校准图）\n- ECE Heatmap（方法 × 数据集 ΔECE）\n- T* Scatter Plot（任务 Regime 可视化）\n\n**重要**：这些模板是起点不是终点。每张图根据 `narrative_intent` 必须加 L3 中的相关 pattern，不能直接套用。\n\n### 主图（Figure 1）特别提示\n\n大多数 ML 论文的 Figure 1 是概念图/架构图/方法概览——正是 PaperBanana 的强项。除非 Figure 1 是纯数值结果对比（如 Pareto frontier），否则优先用 PaperBanana。Figure 1 的 `narrative_intent` 必须与 `writing_plan.yaml` 的 `meta.core_claim` 直接关联。\n\n### PaperBanana 用法\n\n见 `skills/global/paper-banana.skill.md`。意图描述时直接引用 `narrative_intent`，并把 L3 中的 pattern 名写进 intent（如 \"with collapse zone background band, hollow markers for n<50\"）。\n\n---\n\n## 给 Paper Agent 的填写规则\n\n规划 `visualization_plan` 时，对每张图填：\n\n```yaml\n- fig_id: fig2_main_result\n  type: statistical\n  tool: matplotlib\n  data_source: \"registry: exp_001, exp_002\"\n  placement: experiments\n\n  # 新字段（必填）\n  narrative_intent: \"Quantized models match FP16 on accuracy but degrade calibration; ECE gap widens with bits→2.\"\n  design_notes:\n    - \"Pattern A: regime bands for bits=8/4/3\"\n    - \"Pattern C: dashed line for FP16 ECE baseline\"\n    - \"Pattern D: hollow markers for n<50 data points\"\n\n  aspect_ratio: \"4:3\"\n```\n\n`narrative_intent`：一句话，主动语态，描述读者应该相信的事。\n\n`design_notes`：从 L3 选 0-4 个 pattern，给出选用理由。可以为空（说明默认模板足够）。\n\n---\n\n## 提交前 Checklist（以下为统计图专用）\n\nL1 部分（防丑底线）：\n- [ ] 所有轴有 label + 单位\n- [ ] 无 dual y-axis、无 3D、无 pie\n- [ ] 颜色绑定语义、色盲友好\n- [ ] Bar chart 从 0 起（如有）\n- [ ] 字号 ≥ 10pt（缩到 column width 后仍可读）\n- [ ] PDF 矢量、300 DPI\n\nL2 部分（叙事强度）：\n- [ ] 能用一句话写出 figure takeaway（应等于 `narrative_intent`）\n- [ ] Subtitle / panel label 是 insight，不是描述\n- [ ] 数据质量差异在视觉上可见（n、OOS、pending 等）\n- [ ] 至少考虑过是否需要 background band / fill_between / reference line（不一定要加）\n\nL3 部分（高级感——不可跳过）：\n- [ ] 不像 matplotlib gallery 示例（无默认灰背景、无粗网格、无 tab10 色板）\n- [ ] 配色 muted 和谐，不刺眼，不过度饱和\n- [ ] 线宽、字号、间距全图统一\n- [ ] 元素对齐严格（bars 底部对齐、labels 水平对齐）\n- [ ] 留白充足（元素之间不拥挤）\n- [ ] 与论文全文其他图的风格一致\n- [ ] 终极判断：这张图放在 NeurIPS best paper 里违和吗？\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QnXdq51UwWumP6GBJsxPa8`</summary>

```text
1	The detect-and-reweight paradigm~\citep{drayson2025detection} has emerged as a response to synthetic contamination in web corpora, now estimated at 20--60\% of crawled data~\citep{cheng2025contamination}.
2	Training on such data risks model collapse~\citep{shumailov2024model,dohmatob2025strong}, motivating methods that downweight likely-synthetic samples via $w(x) = (1 - q(x))^b$, where $q(x)$ is a detector's synthetic probability and $b$ is a sharpening exponent.
3	An alternative, Detect and Remove (D\&R), hard-filters samples above a threshold~\citep{feng2025beyond}.
4	
5	Despite growing adoption, the paradigm's effectiveness conditions are poorly characterized.
6	Does D\&R genuinely outperform soft reweighting, or do published comparisons confound filtering quality with multi-epoch exposure?
7	What detector properties are necessary for effective reweighting?
8	How does the reweighting mechanism behave across contamination levels, and can $b$ be set without per-model grid search?
9	
10	We investigate these questions through controlled experiments on Qwen3-0.6B continued pretraining (132M FineWeb tokens, $\rho{=}0.4$, 5 seeds), with extensions to $\rho \in \{0.2, 0.6\}$ and 1.7B parameters (\S\ref{sec:limitations} discusses scope).
11	To ground the analysis of exponent selection, we implement a dual-constrained formulation as an illustrative application: find the minimum $b$ such that $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}(b) \geq \tau$ (quality floor, motivated by convergence theory~\citep{wang2026survive}), subject to $\mathrm{ESS}(b) \geq n_{\min}$ (stability ceiling)~\citep{kish1965survey,vehtari2024psis}.
12	
13	Our analysis yields three findings:
14	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
15	\item \textbf{Epoch confound in D\&R evaluation.}
16	The bulk of D\&R's PPL advantage over adaptive reweighting (${>}$95\%) is attributable to multi-epoch exposure, not filtering quality; epoch-matched controls ($N{=}3$) reduce the gap from 0.396 to $\Delta{=}0.017$ (Table~\ref{tab:epoch_match}).
17	
18	\item \textbf{Detector requirements for effective reweighting.}
19	Both sufficient discrimination (AUC) and good calibration (ECE) are empirically observed requirements in our self-contamination setting; a high-AUC but poorly calibrated detector degrades performance below uniform (3 detectors, 3 seeds; \S\ref{sec:robustness}).
20	
21	\item \textbf{Minimum intervention outperforms maximization.}
22	An $\aeff$ quality constraint binds in ${\sim}$50\% of batches at ${\approx}5.9{\times}$ lower sharpening exponents than an ESS-only alternative (\S\ref{sec:regime}); at $\rho{=}0.6$, ESS-only collapses toward uniform while the dual constraint maintains its advantage.
23	\end{itemize}
24	To demonstrate the minimum-intervention principle, we derive a dual-constrained adaptive $\bstar$ that eliminates per-model grid search ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$ at $\rho{=}0.4$, $-$0.032 at $\rho{=}0.6$; Table~\ref{tab:main}), with transferable defaults ($\tau{=}0.7$, $\nmin{=}\lfloor B/2 \rfloor$).
25	The method fails at $\rho{=}0.2$; at 1.7B it shows no degradation but the benefit diminishes.
26	
27	\begin{figure}[t]
28	\centering
29	\definecolor{cblue}{HTML}{0072B2}%
30	\definecolor{corange}{HTML}{E69F00}%
31	\definecolor{cgreen}{HTML}{009E73}%
32	\begin{tikzpicture}[
33	  node distance=0.25cm,
34	  topbox/.style={draw=black!25, rounded corners=3pt, fill=black!6, text width=\columnwidth-1cm, align=center, inner sep=4pt, font=\footnotesize},
35	  fbox/.style={draw=black!15, rounded corners=3pt, fill=#1, text width=\columnwidth-1.1cm, align=left, inner xsep=3pt, inner ysep=3pt, font=\scriptsize},
36	  botbox/.style={draw=cgreen!40!black!20, rounded corners=3pt, fill=cgreen!4, text width=\columnwidth-1cm, align=center, inner sep=4pt, font=\footnotesize},
37	  arr/.style={-{Latex[length=2mm]}, black!30, semithick}
38	]
39	\node[topbox] (top) {\textbf{Detect-and-Reweight}: $w{=}(1{-}q)^b$\quad{\scriptsize[$b$ via grid search]}};
40	\node[fbox=cblue!4, below=of top] (f1) {\textbf{F1.} Epoch confound: ${>}$95\% of D\&R advantage over reweighting is multi-epoch exposure (\S\ref{sec:main-results})};
41	\node[fbox=corange!5, below=0.10cm of f1] (f2) {\textbf{F2.} Detector requirements: need both AUC \emph{and} calibration (\S\ref{sec:robustness})};
42	\node[fbox=cgreen!5, below=0.10cm of f2] (f3) {\textbf{F3.} Mechanism: minimum intervention outperforms maximization (\S\ref{sec:regime})};
43	\node[botbox, below=of f3] (bot) {\textbf{Adaptive $\bstar$}: dual-constrained, no grid search\quad{\scriptsize$\Delta{=}{-}0.023$}};
44	\draw[line width=2.5pt, cblue] ([xshift=0.5pt]f1.north west) -- ([xshift=0.5pt]f1.south west);
45	\draw[line width=2.5pt, corange] ([xshift=0.5pt]f2.north west) -- ([xshift=0.5pt]f2.south west);
46	\draw[line width=2.5pt, cgreen] ([xshift=0.5pt]f3.north west) -- ([xshift=0.5pt]f3.south west);
47	\draw[arr] (top) -- (f1);
48	\draw[arr] (f3) -- (bot);
49	\end{tikzpicture}
50	\caption{Analysis overview. We identify three effectiveness conditions for detect-and-reweight, from which an adaptive $\bstar$ follows as corollary (\S\ref{sec:method}).}
51	\label{fig:overview}
52	\end{figure}
53

_source line 25 · 2026-05-24T09:40:13.311Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PspEGJL1FhKnqt98vG7NL1`</summary>

```text
1	Let $\mathcal{D} = \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{real}} \cup \mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{synth}}$ denote a pretraining corpus with token-level contamination ratio $\rho = |\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{synth}}| / |\mathcal{D}|$, assumed unknown~\citep{cheng2025contamination}.
2	A detector assigns each sample $x$ a synthetic probability $q(x) \in [0,1]$; following \citet{drayson2025detection}, we weight each sample as $w(x) = (1 - q(x))^{b}$, where $b \geq 0$ is a sharpening exponent ($b{=}0$ recovers uniform training).
3	Rather than selecting $b$ via grid search~\citep{drayson2025detection}, we derive $b$ per-batch from the batch's $q$-score distribution using two opposing constraints.
4	
5	Given a batch $\mathcal{B} = \{x_1, \ldots, x_B\}$ with scores $q_i = q(x_i)$ and weights $w_i(b) = (1 - q_i)^{b}$:
6	
7	\paragraph{Quality floor.}
8	The \emph{effective authenticity} $\aeff(b) = \sum_i w_i(b)(1{-}q_i) / \sum_i w_i(b)$ measures the weighted mean detector confidence that samples are real.
9	We require $\aeff(b) \geq \tau$, yielding a lower bound $b_\tau$ on the exponent, motivated by convergence theory under contamination~\citep{wang2026survive}.
10	$\aeff$ correlates with the true batch-level real fraction at Pearson $r \approx 0.79$ (Appendix~\ref{app:alpha_validation}); limitations are discussed in \S\ref{sec:discussion}.
11	
12	\paragraph{Stability ceiling.}
13	Kish's effective sample size $\mathrm{ESS}(b) = (\sum_i w_i)^2 / \sum_i w_i^2$~\citep{kish1965survey} decreases monotonically in $b$ (Appendix~\ref{app:proofs}).
14	We require $\mathrm{ESS}(b) \geq \nmin$, yielding an upper bound $\bess$.
15	
16	\paragraph{Adaptive $\bstar$.}
17	Since $\aeff$ is non-decreasing and ESS is decreasing in $b$, the two constraints define an interval $[b_\tau, \bess]$.
18	The optimal exponent implements \emph{minimum intervention}:
19	\begin{equation}
20	  b^{*} = \begin{cases}
21	    0        & \text{if } \aeff(0) \geq \tau, \\
22	    b_\tau   & \text{if } b_\tau \leq \bess, \\
23	    \bess & \text{otherwise (stability precedence)},
24	  \end{cases}
25	  \label{eq:bstar}
26	\end{equation}
27	applying the smallest $b$ that achieves the quality floor.
28	Each batch falls into one of three regimes---clean, $\aeff$-binding, or ESS-binding (Figure~\ref{fig:dual_constraint})---whose frequencies we analyze in \S\ref{sec:regime}.
29	
30	\begin{figure*}[t]
31	\centering
32	\includegraphics[width=\textwidth]{dual_constraint_illustration}
33	\caption{Dual-constraint $\bstar$ selection for two representative batches ($B{=}4$, $\tau{=}0.7$, $\nmin{=}2$).
34	\textbf{(a)}~Quality constraint binds (${\sim}$50\% of batches): $\aeff(b)$ reaches $\tau$ before $\mathrm{ESS}(b)$ drops below $\nmin$, so $\bstar = \btau$ (minimum intervention).
35	\textbf{(b)}~Stability constraint binds (${\sim}$44\%): $\mathrm{ESS}(b)$ hits the floor before $\aeff$ reaches $\tau$, so $\bstar = \bess$ (stability precedence).
36	Green shading marks the feasible interval $[\btau, \bess]$; red marks over-sharpening.}
37	\label{fig:dual_constraint}
38	\end{figure*}
39	
40	Given $\bstar$, normalized weights $\pi_i = w_i(\bstar) / \sum_j w_j(\bstar)$ define the weighted loss $\mathcal{L} = \sum_i \pi_i \ell_i$; $\bstar$ is found via bisection (Algorithm~\ref{alg:bstar}, Appendix~\ref{app:algorithm}).
41	Defaults: $\tau{=}0.7$, $\nmin{=}\lfloor B/2 \rfloor$; sensitivity in \S\ref{sec:sensitivity}.
42	
```

### Tool result

_source line 27 · 2026-05-24T09:40:13.797Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PYM1uiyqCkyiUovDzxTMEM`</summary>

```text
1	\paragraph{Training Data.}
2	We sample a 132M-token subset of FineWeb~\citep{penedo2024fineweb} as the real-data source, comparable to typical domain-adaptive continued pretraining budgets~\citep{gururangan2020dont}.
3	To simulate web-scale synthetic contamination, we generate continuations with Qwen3-0.6B~\citep{yang2025qwen3}, the same architecture used for continued pretraining, and inject them at token-level ratio $\rho = 0.4$ via uniform random mixing.\footnote{$\rho$ denotes the token-level contamination ratio. Because synthetic documents are shorter on average (304 vs.\ 560 words), the document-level synthetic fraction is 55.6\% at $\rho{=}0.4$.}
4	This self-contamination design means generator and trainee share the same distribution; implications for detection difficulty are discussed in \S\ref{sec:limitations}.
5	Our self-contamination design provides a controlled testbed with known ground-truth labels but limits ecological validity; cross-model transfer results (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_model}) are mixed, and we discuss this limitation in \S\ref{sec:limitations}.
6	
7	\paragraph{Detector.}
8	We train a TF-IDF + Logistic Regression classifier on a held-out labeled split (AUC\,=\,0.94, calibrated ECE\,=\,0.05 after temperature scaling with $T{=}0.204$).
9	The resulting $q$-scores have mean 0.60, standard deviation 0.37, and median 0.71, with 46.3\% of samples in the uncertain range $[0.2, 0.8]$.
10	We use this lightweight detector rather than neural alternatives (e.g., ModernBERT at AUC\,${\geq}$\,0.98 in \citealt{drayson2025detection}) to test whether score \emph{calibration} may matter as much as raw discrimination for reweighting.
11	To evaluate detector sensitivity, we additionally test two generative baselines: a GPT-2-based perplexity scorer (AUC\,=\,0.91) and an N-gram language model perplexity scorer (AUC\,=\,0.73); results are reported in \S\ref{sec:robustness} (implementation details in Appendix~\ref{app:detector_details}).
12	
13	\paragraph{Training.}
14	We continue pretraining Qwen3-0.6B from its pretrained weights for 6{,}103 steps (micro-batch size $B{=}4$, gradient accumulation 4, effective batch 16) with AdamW, linear schedule with warmup (peak lr\,=\,$5{\times}10^{-5}$), max sequence length 2048, and bf16 precision on a single NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (96\,GB).
15	We use $B{=}4$ as the default micro-batch size, reflecting memory constraints typical of continued pretraining setups; \S\ref{sec:sensitivity} demonstrates that larger batches amplify the adaptive advantage.
16	All main comparisons use three random seeds (42, 123, 456).
17	
18	\paragraph{Evaluation.}
19	We report held-out perplexity (PPL) on 5{,}000 documents from a FineWeb holdout set ($\sim$4M tokens) containing only human-authored text, via sliding-window evaluation (stride 1024).
20	All evaluations are post-hoc with a unified pipeline; training involves no inline evaluation.
21	
22	\paragraph{Methods.}
23	We compare six approaches:
24	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
25	  \item \textbf{Uniform}: $w_j = 1$ for all $j$ (no contamination handling).
26	  \item \textbf{Grid-$b$}: fixed $b \in \{0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0, 5.0\}$ (nine candidates), best selected post-hoc by held-out PPL.
27	        Represents validation-based selection~\citep{drayson2025detection}.
28	  \item \textbf{ESS-only}: $b^* = \max\{b : \mathrm{ESS}(b) \geq n_{\min}\}$, the maximum-intervention variant retaining only the stability constraint, ablating $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
29	  \item \textbf{Golden Ratio}: $w = 1/\varphi \approx 0.618$ for synthetic ($q_j > 0.5$), $w = 1$ otherwise~\citep{he2025golden}.
30	        Requires known provenance labels (oracle).
31	  \item \textbf{Adaptive} (ours): per-batch $b^*$ from the dual-constraint formulation (\S\ref{sec:method}), with $\tau = 0.7$ (default; $\tau{=}0.8$ comparable, \S\ref{sec:sensitivity}) and $n_{\min} = 2$ (half the micro-batch size).
32	  \item \textbf{Detect \& Remove}: hard filtering that discards samples with $q_j > 0.5$ and trains uniformly on the remainder.
33	        Requires a calibrated detection threshold; threshold sensitivity is analyzed in \S\ref{sec:main-results}.
34	\end{itemize}
35	
```

```text
1	\subsection{Main Results}
2	\label{sec:main-results}
3	
4	Table~\ref{tab:main} reports held-out perplexity at $\rho = 0.4$ across five seeds.
5	Continued pretraining reduces PPL from 22.970 to the 17.3--17.8 range; method comparisons operate within a narrow residual.
6	
7	Adaptive $\bstar$ achieves 5-seed mean PPL 17.674, compared to 17.697 for uniform ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$, directionally consistent across all seeds) and 17.620 for grid $b{=}0.3$ (3-seed mean; gap $+$0.054).
8	A paired $t$-test yields $t(4){=}{-}8.21$, $p{=}0.0012$; after Bonferroni--Holm correction for nine family-wise comparisons, $p_{\mathrm{BH}}{=}0.011$, reaching conventional significance at $\alpha{=}0.05$.
9	The engineering benefit is eliminating $b$ grid search (nine full training runs~\citep{drayson2025detection}, ${\sim}$16 GPU-hours at 0.6B scale, scaling linearly with model size), though grid search yields a larger improvement.
10	The grid-best $b{=}0.3$ is a post-hoc oracle from nine candidates with $5.5{\times}$ higher cross-seed standard deviation than adaptive ($\sigma{=}0.033$ vs.\ $0.006$); the noisy PPL plateau at $b \in [0.1, 0.5]$ (Figure~\ref{fig:grid_b_trend}) makes validation-based selection unreliable.
11	At the nearest fixed point ($b{=}1.0$), PPL equals uniform (17.692 vs.\ 17.694): per-batch adaptation is essential.
12	
13	The ablation hierarchy at $\rho{=}0.4$ (Adaptive ${<}$ Uniform ${<}$ ESS-only ${<}$ Random, Table~\ref{tab:main}) reveals two requirements for effective reweighting: detector signal and minimum intervention.
14	Random weights $\mathcal{U}(0,2)$ (17.802) confirm that detector $q$-scores carry genuine provenance signal; uninformed reweighting only increases gradient variance.
15	ESS-only (mean $17.737 {\pm} 0.021$) uses the same $q$-scores but maximizes $b$ up to the stability ceiling, concentrating weights on a few high-confidence samples; it degrades PPL by 0.043 relative to uniform.
16	Only the dual-constrained adaptive method, which limits $b$ to the minimum needed, improves in-distribution PPL over uniform (mean $\Delta{=}{-}0.023$, directionally consistent across all seeds).
17	Figure~\ref{fig:grid_b_trend} contextualizes both results: adaptive $\bstar$ operates near the curve minimum while ESS-only $\bstar$ (mean ${\approx}$3.5) falls in the degradation zone.
18	
19	\paragraph{Hard filtering and the epoch confound.}
20	Detect \& Remove at its best threshold ($q{>}0.3$) achieves 3-seed mean PPL $17.250 \pm 0.001$ at full training length, apparently outperforming all reweighting methods.
21	However, filtering retains only 31.5\% of tokens, so the model sees surviving data ${\sim}$3.2 \emph{content epochs} versus one for reweighting.
22	An epoch-matched control (D\&R at $q{>}0.5$, 2{,}533 steps, ${\sim}$1.0 content epochs) yields PPL $17.657 \pm 0.005$ vs.\ $17.674 \pm 0.007$ for adaptive $\bstar$ ($\Delta{=}0.017$); the bulk of D\&R's full-length advantage (0.396 PPL) is attributable to multi-epoch exposure (Table~\ref{tab:epoch_match}).
23	The raw threshold--PPL curve is U-shaped ($q{>}0.3$: 17.25, $q{>}0.9$: 17.42), but epoch-matched comparisons eliminate the U-shape, revealing monotonically decreasing PPL that saturates at $q{\geq}0.5$ (Figure~\ref{fig:dr_dual_curve}, Appendix~\ref{app:hybrid}): the apparent optimum at $q{>}0.3$ reflects maximally compensating multi-epoch exposure, not filtering quality.
24	
25	\paragraph{LR-controlled verification.}
26	To rule out the LR confound (epoch-matched D\&R inherits the full-length schedule's LR trajectory), we retrain D\&R ($q{>}0.5$) and uniform with independent linear schedules (2{,}533 steps each, $\mathrm{LR} \to 0$).
27	D\&R achieves $17.651$, matching the shared-schedule result ($17.657$, $\Delta{=}0.006$); uniform achieves $18.062$, confirming the epoch-exposure advantage ($\Delta{=}{-}0.411$) persists under controlled LR.
28

31	\small
32	\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}

34	\textbf{Method} & \textbf{Steps} & \textbf{Epochs} & \textbf{PPL} $\downarrow$ \\
35	\midrule
36	D\&R full ($q{>}0.5$) & 6{,}103 & ${\sim}$2.4 & 17.278 $\pm$ .007 \\
37	D\&R epoch-matched & 2{,}533 & ${\sim}$1.0 & 17.657 $\pm$ .005 \\
38	Adaptive $\bstar$  & 6{,}103 & ${\sim}$1.0 & 17.674 $\pm$ .007 \\

41	\caption{Epoch-matched comparison ($\rho{=}0.4$, 3 seeds).

43	Note: epoch-matched D\&R shares the full-length LR schedule; an independent-schedule replication (D\&R $17.651$, uniform $18.062$) matches the shared-schedule result ($\Delta{=}0.006$), confirming the epoch-exposure advantage persists under controlled LR.}

86	The shaded region marks $b \geq 2$ where PPL degrades past the uniform baseline; ESS-only $\bstar$ (overall mean ${\approx}$3.5) falls in this degradation zone, while adaptive $\bstar$ (mean ${\approx}$0.9) operates near the curve minimum.}
87	\label{fig:grid_b_trend}

89	
90	
91	\subsection{Robustness and Boundary Conditions}
92	\label{sec:robustness}
93	
94	Across three seeds at $\rho = 0.6$, adaptive $\bstar$ ($17.731 \pm 0.004$) outperforms uniform on in-distribution PPL ($17.763 \pm 0.008$, $\Delta{=}{-}0.032$, paired $t{=}{-}9.95$, $p{=}0.010$\footnote{Computed from 3dp-rounded per-seed values (Table~\ref{tab:rho06}) for reproducibility. Under full-family Bonferroni--Holm correction ($k{=}9$), $p_{\mathrm{BH}}{=}0.064$; directionally consistent across all seeds.}), exceeding the $\rho{=}0.4$ improvement ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$).
95	The ablation hierarchy is preserved (Grid 17.634\footnote{Single seed; full per-seed results in Appendix~\ref{app:rho06}.} ${<}$ Adaptive 17.731 ${<}$ Uniform 17.763 ${\approx}$ ESS-only 17.763 ${<}$ Random 17.861), but the ESS-only/uniform gap collapses to effectively zero (three-seed means both $17.763$) as ESS binding rises to ${\sim}$49\%.
96	Random remains substantially worse than uniform (gap 0.098, $\sigma{=}0.025$): detector signal is load-bearing even when the stability constraint dominates; the adaptive method's continued advantage is driven by the $\aeff$ quality constraint, which operates independently of the ESS ceiling.
97	Three-seed paired comparison confirms $\tau{=}0.7$ outperforms $\tau{=}0.8$ on both holdout and OOD metrics (\S\ref{sec:sensitivity}).
98	
99	\paragraph{Scale.}
100	At 1.7B parameters (Qwen3-1.7B, $\rho{=}0.4$, $\tau{=}0.8$, 3 seeds), the method scales without degradation but the benefit diminishes.
101	Adaptive $\bstar$ achieves holdout PPL $13.26$ vs.\ uniform $13.27$ ($\Delta{=}{-}0.013$); OOD results are partially consistent (OWT $\Delta{=}{-}0.008$, Wiki $\Delta{=}{+}0.013$) and downstream is unchanged ($\Delta{\approx}{+}0.002$), consistent with larger models being more robust to 0.6B-generated contamination.
102	\paragraph{Out-of-distribution evaluation.}
103	To test whether the in-distribution improvement reflects distribution matching, we evaluate all three from-scratch seeds on OpenWebText and Wikipedia.
104	At $\rho{=}0.4$, adaptive reweighting improves mean PPL on all three evaluation domains: FineWeb $\Delta{=}{-}0.023$ (5 seeds), OpenWebText $\Delta{=}{-}0.028$, Wikipedia $\Delta{=}{-}0.029$ (3 seeds each) (downstream accuracy identical at 0.5414).
105	OOD improvements exceed the in-distribution gain, indicating genuine quality improvement rather than distribution matching toward the training mixture ($\tau{=}0.8$'s OOD effect is smaller; $\tau{=}0.7$'s advantage reflects active OOD improvement, \S\ref{sec:sensitivity}).
106	
107	\paragraph{Calibration, not just discrimination.}
108	Replacing TF-IDF+LR with generative perplexity detectors reverses the advantage across three seeds (GPT-2: AUC\,=\,0.91, ECE\,=\,0.131, $\Delta{=}{+}0.098$; N-gram: AUC\,=\,0.73, ECE\,=\,0.069, $\Delta{=}{+}0.129$; Appendix~\ref{app:detector}).
109	The TF-IDF vs.\ GPT-2 contrast (comparable AUC, $2.7{\times}$ ECE gap) isolates calibration as an empirically observed requirement in our self-contamination setting; N-gram's better ECE (0.069) does not compensate for insufficient AUC (0.73), confirming that both discrimination and calibration are required.
110	
111	
112	\subsection{Hyperparameter Sensitivity}
113	\label{sec:sensitivity}
114	
115	\paragraph{Quality threshold $\tau$.}
116	$\tau{=}0.7$ outperforms $\tau{=}0.8$ on holdout at both contamination levels ($\rho{=}0.4$: 17.674 vs.\ 17.683; $\rho{=}0.6$: paired $t{=}{-}4.70$, $p{<}0.05$; \S\ref{sec:robustness}) and achieves larger OOD gains ($\rho{=}0.4$: $\Delta{=}{-}0.028$/$-$0.029 vs.\ $-$0.005/$-$0.009; Table~\ref{tab:tau}, Appendix~\ref{app:tau_rho}).
117	$\tau \in [0.5, 0.8]$ all improve holdout over uniform, while $\tau {>} 0.8$ degrades (Appendix~\ref{app:tau_sweep}).
118	We also tested per-batch adaptive $\tau$ clamping, which did not improve over fixed thresholds (Appendix~\ref{app:batch_adaptive}).
119	
120	\paragraph{ESS floor $\nmin$.}
121	The default $\nmin{=}2$ ($\nmin/B{=}0.5$) is optimal; extremes degrade ($\nmin{=}1$: PPL $17.742{\pm}0.009$, 3 seeds, worse than uniform; $\nmin{=}B$: recovers uniform).
122	Neither constraint alone improves over uniform ($\nmin{=}1$: $+$0.048; ESS-only: $+$0.043), confirming the dual constraint is necessary.
123	The coupling $\nmin{=}\lfloor B/2 \rfloor$ is also empirically motivated: at $B{=}8$, keeping $\nmin{=}2$ (ratio $0.25$) degrades PPL by $+$0.036 vs.\ uniform, whereas $\nmin{=}4$ (ratio $0.5$) yields $\Delta{=}{-}0.052{\pm}0.007$.
124	Under this coupling, the adaptive advantage monotonically increases across $B \in \{4, 8, 16\}$: $\Delta{=}{-}0.023{\pm}0.006$ (5 seeds), $-$0.052${\pm}$0.007 (3 seeds, $p{=}0.006$), and $-$0.068${\pm}$0.005 (3 seeds), corresponding to $1.0{\times}$, $2.3{\times}$, and $3.0{\times}$ amplification ($p{<}0.01$ at $B \geq 8$).
125	The gap to grid-best narrows from 0.040 ($B{=}4$) to 0.009 ($B{=}16$, seed\,42).
126	
127	\paragraph{Constraint regime.}
128	\label{sec:regime}
129	Over 24{,}412 micro-batches (seed\,42), 5.8\% are clean ($\aeff(0) \geq \tau$, $\bstar{=}0$), ${\sim}$50\% are $\aeff$-binding (quality constraint dominates), and ${\sim}$44\% are ESS-binding (stability constraint dominates); the distribution is stable across seeds (CV ${<}2\%$; Appendix~\ref{app:regime}).
130	The $\aeff$-binding majority indicates that detector quality signal---not training stability---is the primary bottleneck, reinforcing the calibration requirement (\S\ref{sec:robustness}).
131

cross-seed markers at $b{=}0.5$ (triangles) confirm the plateau region.\nThe shaded region marks $b \\geq 2$ where PPL degrades past the uniform baseline; ESS-only $\\bstar$ (overall mean ${\\approx}$3.5) falls in this degradation zone, while adaptive $\\bstar$ (mean ${\\approx}$0.9) operates near the curve minimum.}\n\\label{fig:grid_b_trend}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\n\\subsection{Robustness and Boundary Conditions}\n\\label{sec:robustness}\n\nAcross three seeds at $\\rho = 0.6$, adaptive $\\bstar$ ($17.731 \\pm 0.004$) outperforms uniform on in-distribution PPL ($17.763 \\pm 0.008$, $\\Delta{=}{-}0.032$, paired $t{=}{-}9.95$, $p{=}0.010$\\footnote{Computed from 3dp-rounded per-seed values (Table~\\ref{tab:rho06}) for reproducibility. Under full-family Bonferroni--Holm correction ($k{=}9$), $p_{\\mathrm{BH}}{=}0.064$; directionally consistent across all seeds.}), exceeding the $\\rho{=}0.4$ improvement ($\\Delta{=}{-}0.023$).\nThe ablation hierarchy is preserved (Grid 17.634\\footnote{Single seed; full per-seed results in Appendix~\\ref{app:rho06}.} ${<}$ Adaptive 17.731 ${<}$ Uniform 17.763 ${\\approx}$ ESS-only 17.763 ${<}$ Random 17.861), but the ESS-only/uniform gap collapses to effectively zero (three-seed means both $17.763$) as ESS binding rises to ${\\sim}$49\\%.\nRandom remains substantially worse than uniform (gap 0.098, $\\sigma{=}0.025$): detector signal is load-bearing even when the stability constraint dominates; the adaptive method's continued advantage is driven by the $\\aeff$ quality constraint, which operates independently of the ESS ceiling.\nThree-seed paired comparison confirms $\\tau{=}0.7$ outperforms $\\tau{=}0.8$ on both holdout and OOD metrics (\\S\\ref{sec:sensitivity}).\n\n\\paragraph{Scale.}\nAt 1.7B parameters (Qwen3-1.7B, $\\rho{=}0.4$, $\\tau{=}0.8$, 3 seeds), the method scales without degradation but the benefit diminishes.\nAdaptive $\\bstar$ achieves holdout PPL $13.26$ vs.\\ uniform $13.27$ ($\\Delta{=}{-}0.013$); OOD results are partially consistent (OWT $\\Delta{=}{-}0.008$, Wiki $\\Delta{=}{+}0.013$) and downstream is unchanged ($\\Delta{\\approx}{+}0.002$), consistent with larger models being more robust to 0.6B-generated contamination.\n\\paragraph{Out-of-distribution evaluation.}\nTo test whether the in-distribution improvement reflects distribution matching, we evaluate all three from-scratch seeds on OpenWebText and Wikipedia.\nAt $\\rho{=}0.4$, adaptive reweighting improves mean PPL on all three evaluation domains: FineWeb $\\Delta{=}{-}0.023$ (5 seeds), OpenWebText $\\Delta{=}{-}0.028$, Wikipedia $\\Delta{=}{-}0.029$ (3 seeds each) (downstream accuracy identical at 0.5414).\nOOD improvements exceed the in-distribution gain, indicating genuine quality improvement rather than distribution matching toward the training mixture ($\\tau{=}0.8$'s OOD effect is smaller; $\\tau{=}0.7$'s advantage reflects active OOD improvement, \\S\\ref{sec:sensitivity}).\n\n\\paragraph{Calibration, not just discrimination.}\nReplacing TF-IDF+LR with generative perplexity detectors reverses the advantage across three seeds (GPT-2: AUC\\,=\\,0.91, ECE\\,=\\,0.131, $\\Delta{=}{+}0.098$; N-gram: AUC\\,=\\,0.73, ECE\\,=\\,0.069, $\\Delta{=}{+}0.129$; Appendix~\\ref{app:detector}).\nThe TF-IDF vs.\\ GPT-2 contrast (comparable AUC, $2.7{\\times}$ ECE gap) isolates calibration as an empirically observed requirement in our self-contamination setting; N-gram's better ECE (0.069) does not compensate for insufficient AUC (0.73), confirming that both discrimination and calibration are required.\n\n\n\\subsection{Hyperparameter Sensitivity}\n\\label{sec:sensitivity}\n\n\\paragraph{Quality threshold $\\tau$.}\n$\\tau{=}0.7$ outperforms $\\tau{=}0.8$ on holdout at both contamination levels ($\\rho{=}0.4$: 17.674 vs.\\ 17.683; $\\rho{=}0.6$: paired $t{=}{-}4.70$, $p{<}0.05$; \\S\\ref{sec:robustness}) and achieves larger OOD gains ($\\rho{=}0.4$: $\\Delta{=}{-}0.028$/$-$0.029 vs.\\ $-$0.005/$-$0.009; Table~\\ref{tab:tau}, Appendix~\\ref{app:tau_rho}).\n$\\tau \\in [0.5, 0.8]$ all improve holdout over uniform, while $\\tau {>} 0.8$ degrades (Appendix~\\ref{app:tau_sweep}).\nWe also tested per-batch adaptive $\\tau$ clamping, which did not improve over fixed thresholds (Appendix~\\ref{app:batch_adaptive}).\n\n\\paragraph{ESS floor $\\nmin$.}\nThe default $\\nmin{=}2$ ($\\nmin/B{=}0.5$) is optimal; extremes degrade ($\\nmin{=}1$: PPL $17.742{\\pm}0.009$, 3 seeds, worse than uniform; $\\nmin{=}B$: recovers uniform).\nNeither constraint alone improves over uniform ($\\nmin{=}1$: $+$0.048; ESS-only: $+$0.043), confirming the dual constraint is necessary.\nThe coupling $\\nmin{=}\\lfloor B/2 \\rfloor$ is also empirically motivated: at $B{=}8$, keeping $\\nmin{=}2$ (ratio $0.25$) degrades PPL by $+$0.036 vs.\\ uniform, whereas $\\nmin{=}4$ (ratio $0.5$) yields $\\Delta{=}{-}0.052{\\pm}0.007$.\nUnder this coupling, the adaptive advantage monotonically increases across $B \\in \\{4, 8, 16\\}$: $\\Delta{=}{-}0.023{\\pm}0.006$ (5 seeds), $-$0.052${\\pm}$0.007 (3 seeds, $p{=}0.006$), and $-$0.068${\\pm}$0.005 (3 seeds), corresponding to $1.0{\\times}$, $2.3{\\times}$, and $3.0{\\times}$ amplification ($p{<}0.01$ at $B \\geq 8$).\nThe gap to grid-best narrows from 0.040 ($B{=}4$) to 0.009 ($B{=}16$, seed\\,42).\n\n\\paragraph{Constraint regime.}\n\\label{sec:regime}\nOver 24{,}412 micro-batches (seed\\,42), 5.8\\% are clean ($\\aeff(0) \\geq \\tau$, $\\bstar{=}0$), ${\\sim}$50\\% are $\\aeff$-binding (quality constraint dominates), and ${\\sim}$44\\% are ESS-binding (stability constraint dominates); the distribution is stable across seeds (CV ${<}2\\%$; Appendix~\\ref{app:regime}).\nThe $\\aeff$-binding majority indicates that detector quality signal---not training stability---is the primary bottleneck, reinforcing the calibration requirement (\\S\\ref{sec:robustness}).\n",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01KTm6hxebSdjrFuXRxfza1b`</summary>

```text
1	\paragraph{The epoch confound in D\&R evaluation.}
2	The epoch confound identified in \S\ref{sec:main-results} accounts for ${>}$95\% of D\&R's reported advantage; the residual ($\Delta{=}0.017$, 5 seeds) falls within cross-seed variance and is robust to LR schedule choice ($\Delta{=}0.006$ under independent schedules).
3	This confound was invisible in \citet{drayson2025detection} because from-scratch pretraining on large corpora rarely completes even one epoch; in continued pretraining, aggressive D\&R filtering (retaining 31.5\% at $q{>}0.3$) creates ${\sim}$3.2 content epochs, making the confound quantitatively significant.
4	A permuted-score control ($+$0.26 vs.\ uniform) confirms that detector signal is load-bearing.
5	
6	\paragraph{When uniform beats reweighting.}
7	Table~\ref{tab:decision} summarizes three conditions, each necessary for effective reweighting: (i)~sufficient detector discrimination \emph{and} calibration (AUC\,$\geq$\,0.9, ECE\,$\leq$\,0.05; \S\ref{sec:robustness}); (ii)~contamination $\rho \geq 0.4$, below which the method degrades ($+$0.046 at $\rho{=}0.2$) with a sharp transition in $[0.3, 0.4]$ (Figure~\ref{fig:delta_vs_rho}, Appendix~\ref{app:rho03}); (iii)~generator--trainee mismatch (at 1.7B the benefit diminishes to $\Delta{=}{-}0.013$ with OOD mixed).
8	When any condition is violated, uniform is the safer default.
9	
10	\begin{table}[t]
11	\centering
12	\small
13	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
14	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
15	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcll@{}}
16	\toprule
17	\textbf{Condition} & \textbf{Req'd?} & \textbf{Evidence} & \textbf{$\Delta$ PPL} \\
18	\midrule
19	Detector AUC $\geq 0.9$ & Yes & N-gram (0.73) degrades & $+$0.129 \\
20	Detector ECE $\leq 0.05$ & Yes & GPT-2 (0.131) degrades & $+$0.098 \\
21	Contamination $\rho \geq 0.4$ & Yes & $\rho{=}0.2$ degrades & $+$0.046 \\
22	Generator $\neq$ trainee & Rec. & 1.7B: $\Delta{=}{-}0.013$, OOD mixed & --- \\
23	\bottomrule
24	\end{tabular}}
25	\caption{Decision summary for practitioners.
26	$\Delta$ values are relative to uniform (positive = worse).
27	When any required condition is violated, uniform is the safer default.}
28	\label{tab:decision}
29	\end{table}
30	
31	\paragraph{Effect size and downstream impact.}
32	The in-distribution PPL improvement ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$ at $\rho{=}0.4$) operates within a narrow residual (${\sim}$0.07-point window between uniform and grid-best), and does not transfer to downstream accuracy: mean accuracy is identical (0.5414 vs.\ 0.5414, 5 seeds, 5 tasks; Appendix~\ref{app:downstream}), consistent with nonlinear PPL-to-accuracy transfer~\citep{tay2023scale,ye2025mixing}.
33	After accounting for the epoch confound, the paradigm's impact at current scales is modest (Appendix~\ref{app:power}); at $\rho{=}0.6$ the effect roughly doubles ($\Delta{=}{-}0.032$) but this extrapolation is speculative.
34	
35	\paragraph{Limitations of $\aeff$ as a proxy.}
36	The $\aeff$ diagnostic correlates with the true batch-level real fraction at Pearson $r \approx 0.79$, explaining ${\sim}$63\% of variance (Appendix~\ref{app:alpha_validation}).
37	The proxy correlation is stable across seeds ($r(\aeff, \bar{q}) = 0.80 \pm 0.001$, spread ${<}$0.003, Spearman $\rho_s \geq 0.86$; Table~\ref{tab:alpha_corr_perseed}).
38	The remaining ${\sim}$37\% unexplained variance is dominated by detector noise (${\sim}$70\%; TF-IDF accuracy drops to ${\sim}$55\% in the boundary region $q \in [0.4, 0.6]$).
39	Scaling to $B{=}16$ reduces per-batch $\aeff$ variance by $10.25{\times}$ but leaves $R^2$ in the 0.59--0.63 range, confirming the ceiling is set by detector calibration rather than batch size; the adaptive advantage nonetheless increases monotonically ($1.0{\times}$ to $3.0{\times}$; \S\ref{sec:sensitivity}).
40	The $r{=}0.79$ correlation limits targeting precision, particularly at $\rho{=}0.2$ where detector signal-to-noise is insufficient.
41	
42	\paragraph{Theory--proxy gap.}
43	The convergence guarantee of \citet{wang2026survive} requires the \emph{true} real-data fraction to exceed a threshold; our $\aeff \geq \tau$ constraint targets a proxy that correlates at $r \approx 0.79$ with the true fraction.
44	This correlation means the condition holds in expectation, but individual batches may violate it---the ${\sim}$37\% unexplained variance implies that some batches satisfying $\aeff \geq \tau$ have true fractions below $\tau$.
45	The $\nmin$ constraint mitigates this: by capping $b$ at the ESS floor, it prevents the optimizer from concentrating weight on a few samples where the proxy is unreliable, acting as a regularizer against single-batch proxy failures~\citep{owen2013mc}.
46

_source line 33 · 2026-05-24T09:40:15.692Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01BcBbP3NvLFSpJdCfiWYcou`</summary>

```text
1	We identify three effectiveness conditions for detect-and-reweight: (1) D\&R evaluations confound filtering with multi-epoch exposure (${>}$95\% of the advantage); (2) detectors require both discrimination \emph{and} calibration; (3) minimum intervention outperforms unconstrained maximization.
2	A dual-constrained adaptive $\bstar$ demonstrates the minimum-intervention principle, improving over uniform at $\rho \geq 0.4$ ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$, $p_{\mathrm{BH}}{=}0.011$, 5 seeds; $\rho{=}0.6$: $\Delta{=}{-}0.032$, $p{=}0.010$) without tuning, but does not yield measurable downstream gains at 0.6B scale.
3	For practitioners, reweighting is worthwhile only when $\rho \geq 0.4$ and the detector is well-calibrated (Table~\ref{tab:decision}).
4	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
5	
```

</details>

      "content": "We identify three effectiveness conditions for detect-and-reweight: (1) D\\&R evaluations confound filtering with multi-epoch exposure (${>}$95\\% of the advantage); (2) detectors require both discrimination \\emph{and} calibration; (3) minimum intervention outperforms unconstrained maximization.\nA dual-constrained adaptive $\\bstar$ demonstrates the minimum-intervention principle, improving over uniform at $\\rho \\geq 0.4$ ($\\Delta{=}{-}0.023$, $p_{\\mathrm{BH}}{=}0.011$, 5 seeds; $\\rho{=}0.6$: $\\Delta{=}{-}0.032$, $p{=}0.010$) without tuning, but does not yield measurable downstream gains at 0.6B scale.\nFor practitioners, reweighting is worthwhile only when $\\rho \\geq 0.4$ and the detector is well-calibrated (Table~\\ref{tab:decision}).\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\n",
      "numLines": 5,
      "startLine": 1,
      "totalLines": 5
    }
  },

_source line 35 · 2026-05-24T09:40:16.165Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01KHBU7FPpAoHqQ6BcxWZ1qX`</summary>

```text
1	As synthetic text reaches an estimated 20--60\% of web corpora, the detect-and-reweight paradigm (downweighting likely-synthetic samples via $w(x) = (1-q(x))^b$) has gained traction, but its effectiveness conditions remain poorly understood.
2	In a controlled self-contamination setting (Qwen3-0.6B generating and training on its own continuations, 5 seeds), we analyze when and why detect-and-reweight works.
3	
4	We identify three findings.
5	First, an epoch-exposure confound in detect-and-remove evaluation: at matched content epochs, the reported advantage shrinks from 0.396 to 0.017 perplexity ($N{=}3$), revealing that ${>}$95\% of the reported advantage reflects data repetition rather than filtering quality.
6	Second, effective reweighting requires both discrimination and calibration from the detector; a poorly calibrated detector (ECE\,=\,0.131) degrades performance below uniform training, while a calibrated alternative succeeds.
7	Third, minimum intervention---applying the smallest sharpening exponent meeting a quality floor---outperforms unconstrained maximization.
8	
9	To demonstrate the minimum-intervention principle, we derive an adaptive $b^*$ that improves over uniform at $\rho \geq 0.4$ ($\Delta{=}{-}0.023$, corrected $p{=}0.011$; $\rho{=}0.6$: $\Delta{=}{-}0.032$), transitioning from harmful ($\rho{=}0.2$) to beneficial with a sharp boundary in $[0.3, 0.4]$.
10	
```

_source line 37 · 2026-05-24T09:40:16.665Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01N9xRWHcBgPbbrhr7xTmPKk`</summary>

```text
1	\paragraph{Synthetic Contamination and Model Collapse.}
2	Training on recursively generated data leads to irreversible quality degradation (\emph{model collapse})~\citep{shumailov2024model}.
3	\citet{alemohammad2024mad} formalize this as Model Autophagy Disorder and show that quality or diversity inevitably degrades without fresh real data, even when original training data is retained.
4	More critically, \citet{dohmatob2025strong} prove under scaling-law regimes that even a vanishingly small synthetic fraction (e.g., $1/1000$) suffices to trigger collapse, ruling out data scaling as a remedy; \citet{dohmatob2024tale} characterize this as a change of scaling laws.
5	\citet{gerstgrasser2024collapse} show that accumulating real data alongside synthetic data can break the recursion, though at substantial collection cost.
6	\citet{kang2025demystifying} systematically study scaling laws for synthetic data in pretraining, finding that rephrased synthetic data mixed with natural text improves data efficiency but textbook-style data degrades loss on many domains.
7	\citet{cheng2025contamination} survey the broader contamination landscape and confirm that synthetic content now constitutes an estimated 20--60\% of web corpora.
8	Not all synthetic data is harmful: the Phi series~\citep{gunasekar2023textbooks,li2023textbooks2} demonstrates that curated synthetic data can improve training efficiency, but this assumes intentional generation with quality control, unlike the uncontrolled web contamination we address.
9	As language models are deployed at scale for content generation~\citep{radford2019language,brown2020language,openai2023gpt4,dubey2024llama}, these results motivate \emph{contamination-aware} training strategies that explicitly account for synthetic provenance rather than treating all tokens equally.
10	
11	\paragraph{Detection and Filtering of Machine-Generated Text.}
12	Given the severity of contamination, a natural response is to detect and remove synthetic content.
13	DetectGPT~\citep{mitchell2023detectgpt} performs zero-shot detection via probability curvature; Fast-DetectGPT~\citep{bao2024fastdetectgpt} accelerates this with conditional probability curvature; Binoculars~\citep{hans2024binoculars} uses cross-perplexity ratios between two models; Min-$k$\% Prob~\citep{shi2024minkprob} uses the average log-probability of the lowest-$k$\% tokens.
14	All produce continuous confidence scores suitable as input to downstream weighting, including ours.
15	\citet{kirchenbauer2023watermark} propose watermarking as a complementary provenance mechanism.
16	\citet{wu2025survey} survey the LLM-generated text detection landscape---the upstream task on which our provenance detector relies---covering statistical, neural, and watermark-based approaches alongside open challenges such as out-of-distribution robustness; RAID~\citep{dugan2024raid} and M4~\citep{wang2024m4} provide shared benchmarks for robust cross-domain evaluation of such detectors.
17	On the mitigation side, \citet{feng2025beyond} prove that even an imperfect verifier prevents collapse, but apply hard binary filtering that discards borderline samples; \citet{yi2025escaping} extend this with convergence guarantees for verified synthetic data.
18	\citet{zibakhsh2025fortifai} propose truncated cross-entropy loss to implicitly discount high-confidence (likely synthetic) tokens during training.
19	Hard filtering requires a precision--recall threshold; soft reweighting avoids this trade-off by assigning continuous weights.
20	
21	Most directly related to our work, \citet{drayson2025detection} propose $w_j = (1{-}q_j)^b$ for collapse prevention via detect-and-reweight in a from-scratch pretraining setting, where $q_j$ is the detector's synthetic probability.
22	We adopt the same weighting formula but differ in several fundamental ways.
23	\textbf{(i)}~Drayson et al.\ select $b$ via validation-set grid search and fix it throughout training; different model scales require different values ($b{=}10$ for ${\leq}360$M, $b{=}1$ for 1.7B), so the choice is \emph{per-model} and expensive.
24	Our $b^*$ is derived per-batch from dual constraints ($\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}} \geq \tau$, $\mathrm{ESS} \geq n_{\min}$) at zero validation cost.
25	\textbf{(ii)}~Their SIR resampling discretizes the weight distribution; we apply direct loss reweighting with continuous gradients.
26	\textbf{(iii)}~They employ a ModernBERT-150M detector (AUC\,=\,0.986) on WikiText-2 (${\sim}$2M tokens); we show that a lightweight TF-IDF+LR detector (AUC\,=\,0.939) suffices on FineWeb-scale data (132M+ tokens), and both calibration and sufficient discrimination appear required in our setting for effective reweighting.
27	
28	\paragraph{Domain-Level Data Mixing.}
29	A parallel line of work optimizes data composition without targeting synthetic contamination.
30	DoReMi~\citep{xie2023doremi} learns mixture weights via Group DRO~\citep{sagawa2020dro} with a proxy model, DSIR~\citep{xie2023dsir} performs importance resampling with $n$-gram features, and \citet{ye2025mixing} discover predictable functional forms relating mixing ratios to performance.
31	AIOLI~\citep{chen2025aioli} unifies these into an online adaptive framework, while \citet{yang2026mixing} train an RL agent for domain-weight selection.
32	DataComp-LM~\citep{gadre2024datacomp} and OLMo~\citep{groeneveld2024olmo} provide standardized benchmarks and open infrastructure for data curation at scale.
33	ToReMi~\citep{zhu2025toremi} extends domain-level mixing by clustering training data into topics and dynamically adjusting category-level weights based on aggregate learning signals.
34	Continued pretraining~\citep{gururangan2020dont} adapts models to specific domains, a setting where contamination-aware methods are directly applicable.
35	These domain-level approaches do not address instance-level contamination within a domain.
36	
37	\paragraph{Instance-Level Data Selection and Reweighting.}
38	Deduplication at the corpus level~\citep{lee2022dedup,tirumala2024d4} is a prerequisite; data-constrained regimes~\citep{muennighoff2024scaling} and data quality filtering~\citep{longpre2024pretrainers,lozhkov2024fineweb} further shape training distributions.
39	SemDeDup~\citep{abbas2023semdedup} deduplicates via embedding similarity, SoftDedup~\citep{he2024softdedup} downweights high-frequency samples via $n$-gram commonness, and QuRating~\citep{wettig2024qurating} selects high-quality instances via learned quality scores.
40	Curriculum learning~\citep{bengio2009curriculum} and focal loss~\citep{lin2017focal} upweight examples by difficulty, \citet{katharopoulos2018not} accelerate training via importance sampling of high-loss examples, \citet{mindermann2022prioritized} prioritize samples by reducible loss, \citet{ren2018learning} meta-learn per-sample weights for noisy-label robustness, and \citet{sow2025dynamic} derive sample weights from per-sample loss with convergence guarantees.
41	LESS~\citep{xia2024less} selects influential data for instruction tuning, MATES~\citep{yu2024mates} trains data-influence models to dynamically select pretraining samples, \citet{chen2024bull} apply hard sample reweighting during LLM continual pretraining, and \citet{kuo2024weighting} show that differentiated weighting of LLM-generated data improves downstream classification.
42	\citet{thakkar2023selfinfluence} use self-influence scores to reweight pretraining samples, promoting novelty and penalizing redundancy; their PRESENCE method operates on intrinsic training signals rather than provenance.
43	\citet{pruthi2020estimating} trace gradient descent to estimate per-example training data influence, enabling post-hoc attribution but at prohibitive cost for pretraining-scale data.
44	Our reweighting differs from all of these in being driven by \emph{provenance} (detector scores estimating human vs.\ synthetic origin) rather than loss, frequency, quality, or domain membership.
45	
46	\paragraph{Theoretical Foundations.}
47	\citet{he2025golden} derive the optimal real-data weight under known mixture proportions, finding the golden-ratio reciprocal (${\approx}0.62$) optimal when both sources are equally sized.
48	Their result requires the contamination ratio $k$, unavailable in web-scale settings; we use it as an oracle baseline.
49	\citet{wang2026survive} prove that contaminated recursive training converges at a rate governed by the effective real-data fraction $\alpha$; this result provides the theoretical motivation for our quality threshold, since $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}(b) \geq \tau$ guarantees a convergence-rate lower bound.
50	We operationalize this without the known-proportion assumption, estimating $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$ from batch-level statistics online.
51	For the stability constraint, we build on Kish's effective sample size~\citep{kish1965survey} and the Pareto-smoothed importance sampling framework of \citet{vehtari2024psis}.
52	Score calibration~\citep{guo2017calibration} is relevant because the adaptive method operates on $q$-scores: miscalibration systematically biases $\alpha_{\mathrm{eff}}$.
53	\citet{kimura2024iw} provide a broader treatment of importance weighting under distribution shift, within which our provenance-based reweighting can be viewed as a special case of covariate-shift correction where the covariate is synthetic origin probability.
54	\citet{fan2023importance} analyze the variance--bias tradeoff in empirical risk minimization with importance weights, providing theoretical grounding for our ESS stability constraint.
55	
```
